## Rules of Conduct for Public Comment at Council Meetings*

Members of the public may speak at a Council meeting upon recognition by the Mayor. Public comment may only occur during periods designated on the agenda for public comment or a public hearing. A person may speak for up to two (2) minutes during each of the two public comment periods on agenda items or non-agenda items. Walvers of the time requirement may only be granted in the discretion of the Mayor, and Walvers to speak at a time other than a designated comment period may only be granted by the Council. Any person wishing to make a presentation longer than two minutes or requiring audio-visual equipment is asked to contact the City Clerk requesting to appear on a future agenda.

A person may only address Council from the podium. Only one person may occupy the podium at a time. All remarks are to be directed to the Mayor and Council. Speakers are not to engage in direct dialog with other meeting attendees.

Any person who violates the Rules of Conduct, disturbs the peace at the meeting, and/or interferes with the meeting may be warned, ordered to be seated, removed, and/or ticketed.

 $ ^{*} $This summarizes Council Resolution 04-18. Complete Rules, including guidelines for considering waivers, are available in the Council Chambers and from the City Clerk.

## PUBLIC COMMENT

Erik Adams thanked Mayor Kennedy and Councilmember Dilg for meeting with the Girl Scouts and allowing them to learn about government. Each Girl Scout then introduced herself. In attendance was Charlotte from Sayre Elementary School, Sophia from Brummer Elementary, Julia from Brummer Elementary, Laney from Kent Lake Elementary, Catherine from Kent Lake Elementary, and Natalie from Kent Lake Elementary.

Ryan Cottingham of 601 S Lafayette stated he is asking Council not to approve the West Lake rezoning without conditions that protect all of the wetlands. He stated a few of them attended the Planning

<a id="p4"></a>
*[Page 4 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/2-12-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202402090926490#page=4)* (read by OCR; check the original)

Commission meetings speaking against destroying the wetlands. He stated the development can happen without demolishing the wetlands and the developer in Planning Commission meetings stated the entrance is not needed for safety and the project can move forward without it. He further stated they are adding a new entrance off of Dixboro and they are paving that portion of the road. He stated it isn't necessary. Mr. Cottingin then stated the new entrance will destroy rare and untouched wetlands that have been here longer than any of us. He then said the developer stated at a Planning Commission meeting, they don't plan on replacing the wetlands in the city, but to bank them in the state's wetlands community bank. Our community will lose the wetlands forever and won't be replaced. He then stated if you vote yes to the new entrance, you are voting yes to destroy the wetlands. He is asking Council to ask the developer to remove the road that would damage the wetlands.

Suzann Martin of 602 W Lake Street stated the project blueprints show a boulevard that encroaches on a rare natural valuable resource of the wetlands. She is asking the boulevard to be removed, and the State doesn't want any disturbances to the wetlands. She said the developer stated he could move forward with the development without the West Lake Blvd. She then stated the police and fire headquarters are located less than a 1/10 of a mile from the entrance/exit on Dixboro Road. She then stated wetlands are important for our environment, they control storm water from extreme rain events that are becoming more widespread. She stated wetlands provide storm water storage, recharge for ground water aquifers and filtration, and stormwater is a significant dilutant of runoff. Wetlands provide wildlife habitat, breeding, nesting and feeding grounds for Native American species, migratory birds and plant species. Disruption of the ecosystem causes those animals to become rarer. She then stated there is a concern of winter road salt, automobile fluids, such as oil, silt and dirt that will occur in the boulevard and the runoff into the wetlands. This will alter the chemical process of the wetlands. Automobile fluids will cause heavy metals that do not dissolve, and it leads to oxygen depletion and death to many species. She then stated the city of South Lyon resources should be considered in the form of the wetlands.

Erin of 601 S Lafayette stated she is here to talk about the project at 10 & Dixboro. She said they sat through several presentations by the developer and she heard directly from them that the second exit that will destroy several acres of our pristine wetlands in the city doesn't need to exist for safety or for the project to move forward. They can spare the wetlands and still have their project. She stated she can see clearly how this exit will cause more traffic with the bottle neck entering the city, but there is no reason not to allow the developer to destroy several acres of beautiful wetlands in our community, our current residents will thank you for not creating more traffic jams, but the future residents will not know what they missed. She stated she has heard some of Council have shown care and respect for our environment and she hopes they don't let the developer destroy the wetlands, they are here for profits and she is here to live.

Ms. Jill Scapelliti thanked Council for allowing her to join the board for the DDA.

## FIRE CHIEF REPORT

Fire Chief Thorington stated they have had 89 instances this year so far, it's been a busy month. He then stated all the counter tops are installed and Brian Hayes has done a great job. He then stated they put in for a Firehouse Sub grant for $42,734 for fire turnout gear. He then stated he attended Representative Slotkins webinar, and she offered her assistant William Moore to do an onsite visit and talk about grants. He stated he hopes to have more grant opportunities down the road. He then stated Micah DeMeyer was elected firefighter of the year for 2023, he is an outstanding firefighter, and generally a great person and takes initiative and always positive and part of a lot of facets of the Fire Department. He then stated they have a lot of smoke detectors and carbon monoxide detectors left and reminded city residents to contact them to have them installed.

## POLICE CHIEF REPORT

Chief Baaki stated they now have their new Dodge Durango 2023 and its being used as a Detective car. He then stated he has started budget talks with the City Manager in regards to what is needed in the upcoming year. He then stated they had their department meeting and Officer Ronald Barbour was elected Officer of the year, 22-year veteran and at the top of the list for Sergeant. ## UNFINISHED BUSINESS

### 1. Road Improvement Program, 2024

City Manager Zelenak updated Council on the 2024 Road Improvement Program. He stated Brookfield and Eagle Pointe areas will be the next project in the multi-year road improvement program. He stated this project will entail repairs of certain sections of concrete roads and reconstruction of Brookfield Drive, and reconstruction of all roads within Eagle Pointe subdivision. Preliminary cost estimate for both projects will be approximately $3.5 - $4. Million dollars. He then stated letters have been sent to both subdivisions informing them of the meeting date to discuss the planned improvements. The meeting will be held on February 1st. He further stated at that meeting, they will discuss bid dates, and construction dates as well. There will be a follow-up meeting in April with more detailed information. We are currently gathering topo information on both subs. Councilmember Kurtzweil asked if they are considering Gibson because she has gotten a lot of questions. City Manager Zelenak stated they are not looking into that particular street at the moment, they are looking at streets in subdivisions so that more than one street can be done at a time.

### 2. Westlake Conditional Rezoning

Hannah Smith of CIB planning spoke about the recommendation from the Planning Commission to approve conditional rezoning request by Elevate Land Holdings, LLC. for a portion of the 34-acre parcel. It is south of the existing West Lake Apartments. She stated they are proposing mixed use development 252 new multiple family residential units, and 3 family residential lots the remaining portion of the parcel to be developed would be approximately 64,000 sq feet of industrial development. It is currently zoned 11, which is light industrial. They are asking to conditionally re-zone 24.4-acre portion to RM3 which is multiple family and 1.63-acre portion for R3 as single family residential, and leave the rest as industrial. She then stated what is different from a traditional rezoning, is it comes with an agreement between the city and the applicant. The agreement includes conditions offered by the applicant and agreed to by the city. As a condition of the rezoning, the property cannot be developed into any other land use other than in the agreement. It also includes a time frame for the phases to be included. Ms. Smith then described the 4 steps that are involved with a conditional rezoning, which includes a public hearing, review of rezoning request, site plan by the Planning Commission, then a review and action by Council and if approved the second reading of the zoning ordinance amendment and final approval, and the last step is review and action on final site plan by the Planning Commission. She then reviewed the information that was included in the packet and that is online under Council packets. She further stated they supplied a traffic impact statement. Part of the project is to relocate the entrance with the current apartments to the east so it is lined up with Charleston Park which will minimize traffic conflicts at 10 Mile and Dixboro. She further stated there were concerns at the Planning Commission meetings in regards to the drive to the

<a id="p7"></a>
*[Page 7 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/2-12-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202402090926490#page=7)* (read by OCR; check the original)

wetlands at the site. One of the concerns was the slope around the roadway creating a larger encroachment on the wetlands. In response to the concerns, the developer has provided details and cross sections of the wetland crossing showing headwalls to reduce that slope and minimize the impact as much as possible. They also supplied a letter from the traffic engineer supporting the relocation of the entrance for safety purposes. She further mentioned the property there have been attempts for development of the property a few times, but has sat vacant for about 20 years since it was zoned industrial. Bob Langen then presented a slide show which reflected the proposed development. (which can be found in the council packet on the city's website). Mr. Langen explained the layout of the development. He showed the currently approved and fully occupied West Lake Apartments. He then showed around that development which included the new entry across from Charleston Park. He stated the buildings are setback from the roadway and the wetlands. He stated the only point they come close to the wetlands is the new entrance, and that is .077, less than  $ 1/10^{th} $ of an acre. He stated they are replicating the apartments that are currently there, except after a market review, they are adding 8 ½% 3-bedroom units. They found there was a small demand for 3-bedroom units. He further explained there will also be a combination of garages and storage units for their personal property and cars. He thought that is a good transition from the residential to the industrial. He then stated they are offering to build the first building in phase 1 with the apartments. He stated they will also be paving Dixboro Road the entire length of their property line. He did say we should relocate the Welcome to the City of South Lyon to the corner since the property is now part of the city. He then spoke about their plan to extend a paved pathway to the Huron Valley Trail when the road is paved, and we will build an eastbound path to connect to the sidewalks in the City of South Lyon. He also spoke about the proposed boulevard entrance, and it shows how far along the road you would be before you saw the clubhouse. He stated the original plan had a larger slope, and they changed that to a straight up and down retaining wall with a fence around it, which reduced the impact on the wetlands to less than  $ 1/10^{th} $ of an acre. They respect the wetlands, and they changed that due to the observation that having the entrance as close to the interchange is now is a bad idea. He further stated this has dramatic public safety implications, in the opinion of the engineers and traffic consultants. He then stated they were hesitant to destroy the nice entrance that is there now and build another one, but the long-range public safety is more important. He stated EGLE informally agreed that the public interest in placing the location for the entrance is more important than the  $ 1/10^{th} $ acre of land, and if EGLE won't issue the permit, they can't do it. He then discussed the parking of the apartments. He stated they built the parking for the current West Lake Apartments per ordinance, and have found they are overparked. He stated they increased the open space to make sure they are right parked. The units don't normally attract residents with multiple cars. Some are single or seniors. They engaged the traffic consultant and they came with a good number and so they added open space and they have pickle ball courts, dog park, clubhouse and pool, and gathering spaces for people to hang out. They want to encourage community and getting the parking right is important. They will have EV parking stations, bike racks, community gardens and he think it is a healthy mix of amenities. He further stated the goal for the industrial buildings is to have a good facade for businesses and keep it flexible and keep it looking nice from the street. He then discussed the amount of industrial they decided to offer, and they thought 64,000 sq feet would work. Mr. Langen stated .077 of an acre is considered a minor activity permit by EGLE. He further stated the pipe that will be used to regulate the left side from the right side will be sized so the bottom will contain the soil that is comprised of the wetlands so the animals will be able to use the tunnel for creatures that want to cross it, according to our consultant. He wants to do what they can for the long-term safety of the residents. He stated there are more wetlands there and they are buffering and they will not be impacted. He then stated if the city deems that the wetlands is more important, he will save themselves their money and leave the entrance where it is. They intend on owning the development long-term, they have no intention on selling to corporate businesses. They will maintain ownership.

<a id="p8"></a>
*[Page 8 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/2-12-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202402090926490#page=8)* (read by OCR; check the original)

Mayor Kennedy then asked if the public would like to speak on the subject. Ryan Cottingin stated it is much smaller than last time and it is appreciated they are trying to minimize the impact of the wetlands. He further stated the nature of the wetlands, are they are untouched, and when they put in the road, they will have to take up more than that space, and they will never be the same. He further stated it is a small impact, but we are impacting the entire wetlands.

Suzann Martin asked if they received a permit from EGLE to do this.

Councilmember Dilg stated she doesn't understand the public safety issue and why it needs to be across from Charleston. Mr. Seiber from Seiber Keast Engineering stated it is in regard to the turning movements coming in and out of the intersection. Traffic analysis showed if the drives line up, traffic moves better, and if you move them down, and people are trying to turn left, they are blocking each other. The problem with the current is the distance is too close to Dixboro. There wasn't a choice when phase 1 was built, but based on the safety study, traffic will back up on Dixboro and that drive will be a safety issue if someone is trying to turn left into the apartments, and someone is waiting to turn left onto Dixboro, people will start to try to pass the other person. Studies show you need a certain distance between the intersections. Councilmember Dilg asked about not having an entrance on 10 Mile. Mr. Seiber stated that would put all the traffic onto the Dixboro. The industrial entrance is a separate entrance. He further stated with an apartment complex that large, you need a second access. Councilmember Dilg asked if there was an environmental study done. Mr. Seiber stated we use a wetland consultant and he reviewed it and confirmed it is minimal. He said EGLE visited the site with the consultant, and they agreed that will be the less impact and it aligns with Charleston Park. Councilmember Dilg stated there is a concern on them building that and the impact from the construction. Mr. Seiber stated they can mostly build it on the inside and not the wetland side, but it is a temporary impact, and from EGLE's standpoint, after the construction, it will be returned to its original state. Councilmember Dilg stated we have had some environmental issues in the past with the tube mill, and other areas. She said she would be more comfortable keeping the current entrance. Councilmember Kivell asked if it is flat, or if there is a crown. Mr. Seiber stated it is like E Lake and flat when coming in and the total depth is between 4 and 5 feet. Councilmember Kivell stated you'll have to retain it on the sides, with the salt and slush. Mr. Seiber stated there would be no salt or road oils going into it because that would go into the storm sewer system that is being built along with it. It would be caught in the catch basin in the curb and everything is sent to the stormwater detention basin. There is also oil and gas separators and pre-treatment for sediment. The way it is set up there is no drainage going into the wetlands from the houses or the streets. Everything goes into the large stormwater detention basin on Dixboro road and goes south to the ditch. Councilmember Kivell stated he read they are looking at 96% of parking will prevent them from being parking rich, and the ordinance would put them over. Mr. Langen stated the ordinance would be right on if there were townhomes going in with people that have more cars. Mr. Langen stated they design the parking to 90% and leave 10% for guests, snowplowing and things like that. Councilmember Kivell stated we have had issues with our parking potential and it has been proven to us we don't need parking as much as requested, but we make people show us. Mr. Langen stated the current apartments are full, and most times it is pretty empty. He then stated if they build phase 1, they will come back and pivot on that. Councilmember Bogert stated he is excited about this and it looks like a well thought out project. He stated one of his concerns is not the footprint of the entrance, but that it is bisecting wetlands. He stated he is not sure what the environmental impact would be, but it would be nice if he heard from someone with that is well versed in environmental issues to enlighten Council on that. He then asked if you plow that boulevard, where does the snow go. Mr. Langen stated it is curbed and guttered and he thinks the bulk of it would be captured by the storm water retention system. Councilmember Bogert asked about something to keep the runoff from the dog park.

<a id="p9"></a>
*[Page 9 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/2-12-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202402090926490#page=9)* (read by OCR; check the original)

Mr. Langen stated there will be a 25 ft buffer of grass, and you would hope people would pick up after their pets. Councilmember Kurtzweil congratulated them on a development for a community that is underserviced in the City of South Lyon. There is a reason phase 1 completely filled. Very nice apartment living is a rarity in this town. They are providing a tremendous service and when people say developers are out for profit need to understand they are providing a needed benefit to our community. She then asked how much of the zoned industrial is being used for residential. Mr. Langen stated about 20 acres. Councilmember Kurtzweil stated a lot of the industrial property is wetlands. She stated if the argument is we don't want to damage the wetlands, what would we be able to do if someone came in and purchased the property and wanted to use it for what it is currently zoned for, it would damage the wetlands. Mr. Langen stated you wouldn't be able to destroy the wetlands, but when you develop industrial, you are increasing road space and land space, and it would be more significant. Councilmember Kurtzweil asked how the city ever accepted them to develop the property if most of it is wetlands. Mr. Langen stated in the 90's there were regulations, but you cannot do as much wetland fill as you could then. This is today. Councilmember Kurtzweil stated she has a background in wetland management through Michigan State University's Master Gardener Program, and she is going through recertification this year, it's mostly about wetland work. She stated wetlands can become more valuable over time, and that is what has happened here. It is sustaining many more varieties of nature on that land, than in the 50's or 60's. She further stated more water has come in and the ground has become more moist, more animals have come in either through flight or through nesting. She further stated that is valuable to her because she lives on a wetland and she doesn't use toxins that can drain into the wetlands. She further stated there is always a certain amount of leeching and hopefully they will use earth-friendly products. She said there is a tremendous effort around the city to highly protect the wetlands throughout the city. She further stated the city tends to have the developers understand the wetlands is a priority. She then stated where we don't have wetlands is in the downtown area, this is an opportunity for people in the downtown whether they are riding by or walking by to enjoy the life that comes from the wetlands, and all of that enhances a positive environment. She understands the boulevard, but if they will give it up, she would vote for it. She is an environmentalist and she likes the retaining wall, they don't have one at 9 mile and you can see the runoff from the road. She then stated another issue is the aquafer is there and she doesn't understand why the city didn't purchase the property. She wants to take a protection approach.

Councilmember Hansen asked about discussion about a public bus stop, or where the school bus stop would be. He further stated he was on an HOA before, and will the islands be irrigated because the soil is very hard in South Lyon. He then stated he has concerns about the wetlands, and he would rather see the road not in the wetlands. Mr. Langen stated they felt the traffic impact was more of a big deal, but they are ok with losing it. Councilmember Hansen stated he loves the design and paving Dixboro, but his concern is the wetlands. He has a pond behind his house and they take care of it.

City Manager Zelenak stated one of the requirements in our Ordinance is to have driveways across from each other when there is a development going in. Previously, they didn't own that property so the driveways couldn't be that way. The existing location is 150 feet from Dixboro Road, and the close proximity of people turning left onto W Lake and people turning right on Dixboro Road at 35 MPH, and it's only a couple seconds between that location and the current driveway. When they met with the applicant, they discussed moving it to the east, which would be 8 or 9 seconds between cars. He stated EGLE looks at everything and makes the determination and gives written documentation if they approve or do not approve the entrance but that is after the discussions and approvals with Council has taken place. Mayor Kennedy clarified that EGLE will not officially approve or not approve until after the plans are turned into them which is after approved by Council. They can always come back and say they do not

<a id="p10"></a>
*[Page 10 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/2-12-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202402090926490#page=10)* (read by OCR; check the original)

approve it. Some people think every place with cattails are wetlands, and that isn't the case. There are many types of wetlands in the city, and it is based upon soils and how it was created and when it was created and if it is a significant impact on the city. He further stated Oakland County created wetlands. Mayor Kennedy stated at some point, if that entrance is moved, there is an opportunity for a light to be put in for Charleston Park and this development and they would also be able to walk across the street safely. City Manager Zelenak stated based upon the planners and engineers, you don't want only one entrance for the number of people that will be living there. The Fire Department suggested more than one entrance as well. Councilmember Kurtzweil stated there are at least 2 places in the city that isn't directly across the other development. Same issue on 9 Mile. She stated when you turn out, you have to look both ways. City Manager Zelenak stated we have learned more about how that is unsafe which is why it's in our Ordinance. Councilmember Kurtzweil stated we have all survived and it isn't an issue. City Manager Zelenak stated when you have the opportunity to do so, it should be our best practice, based upon our knowledge and our ordinance. We have to gauge what is more importance, the public safety, or upsetting a small area of wetlands. Councilmember Kivell stated Martindale stacks up as well during the morning and evening, and he understands safety issue, but it is not ideal. Councilmember Dilg stated she still doesn't understand the safety issue. City Manager Zelenak stated vehicles turning in opposite directions never face each other, you never come in contact with each other. He further stated some people don't like round abouts, but that prevents t-boning accidents. He stated that drive that is there now was only designed for the 3 buildings, we are going to have a lot more people coming and going with the new development. Councilmember Bogert asked if it could be a right turn only when leaving the development. Mr. Langen stated that would be a road commission question. Either way, the road commission will have to approve it. Further discussion was held regarding the current entrance. He stated if Council wants to leave the entrance as it is, he will agree to that. Councilmember Kurtzweil stated she can't think of another apartment complex with the backdrop as this. She doesn't want to change the backdrop there. They are creating an outdoor experience. Even that .077 still holds a lot of wildlife and it makes you understand the connection we have with the earth. City Attorney Hamameh stated this is a little different than a planned development. The applicant has to offer the conditions, then Council has to consider the conditions he is offering. Council cannot change his conditions, but the applicant can change them. She further stated what was published for first reading is what is being presented tonight. Discussion was held regarding the motion options Council has. City Manager Zelenak stated the applicant will now go back and make their decisions if they want to make that change, and then it will come before Council again.

CM 1-7-24 MOTION TO TABLE UNTIL FEBRUARY 12, 2024 COUNCIL MEETING

Motion by Hansen, supported by Mosier

Motion to postpone until the February 12th meeting for a new site plan to be brought to Council ROLL CALL VOTE: Kurtzweil- Yes

Hansen-Yes

Dilg- Yes

Bogert-Yes

Kivell-Yes

Mosier- Yes

Kennedy-Yes

MOTION CARRIED UNANIMOUSLY

### 3. City personnel policy

City Manager Zelenak stated that he and the Finance and Benefit Administrator have been working over the past year making changes and improvements to our Employee Personnel Policy. The results of those efforts are included within the proposed policy. The changes were based on employment law and also

<a id="p11"></a>

updating policies that were outdated. Councilmember Bogert thanked him for doing this and it was well thought out and contemporary. Councilmember Kurtzweil asked if the City Manager plans on meeting with the employees since there were changes made. City Manager Zelenak stated he will. Councilmember Mosier stated this was a long time coming, and well thought out and this will bring us into compliance with the law, which is important.

### 4. Fiscal year 2023-2024 budget amendments

City Manager Zelenak stated the fiscal year 2023-2024 budget amendments totaling $38,967 for general fund expenditures, $365,000 for local streets fund expenditures, $4,000 for capital improvement fund expenditures, $477,225 for water and sewer fund expenditures and $118,605 for equipment replacement fund expenditures. And the creation of a new GL account code for CDBG fund. He then stated the majority relates to the Hagadorn road improvement program and some expenditures were supposed to happen in the prior year, and the other was related to a purchase that was delayed through the prior year as well.

##  $ \underline{\text{MANAGER'S REPORT}} $

City Manager Zelenak stated on a future agenda we will be discussing the 318 W Lake Street sale and construction. We will also be setting the budget dates. Washington Manor will also be on a future agenda. He stated we now have added a link for more information on the transit services that reflect the expanded services. He then thanked the DPW and staff for the excellent job they have done with the recent snow storm and conditions. He also wants people to understand at 30 degrees, 1 pound of salt will

<a id="p12"></a>
*[Page 12 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/2-12-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202402090926490#page=12)* (read by OCR; check the original)

melt 46 pounds of ice, and at 20 degrees, 1 pound will only melt 9 pounds of ice, and at 1 degree it will only melt about 4 pounds of ice. Mayor Kennedy then asked about the preliminary discussion that was held regarding the school's millage and Colonial Acres being homestead or non-homestead. City Manager Zelenak stated Colonial Acres are all deemed as homestead properties. Councilmember Kurtzweil stated apartments will be affected, so their rent could be increased if it passes to pass the cost off to them. We have seniors living in the apartments. Also, all the commercial businesses will be affected. She stated this has an inflationary affect. The business owner will pass the cost off to the customers or the tenant in the building. Businesses don't absorb this; they pass it off and we live in an expensive city. Councilmember Dilg asked if it is a renewal of a millage, and is there an increase. City Clerk Deaton stated currently non homestead property owners pay the 18 mills for the school operating which is the higher rate. She then stated the language states it is a renewal, but the language also states they are asking for an additional .7151. Councilmember Kurtzweil stated it is her understanding because the school district had the option of renewing it at 18 mills, because of their financial issues in the school district. If they just did the 18 mills people would have been ok with that, but it is an increase over the renewal.

## COUNCIL COMMENTS

Councilmember Kurtzweil thanked the Girl Scouts that were here and she also already bought some girl scout cookies. She then thanked Julie Paquette for her continuing commitment to the Roper Barn Project and she is working hard to raise this barn and thank her for her generous time she is devoting to preservation efforts in our community. She is having a fundraiser this Friday at the VFW hall and there is a registration fee and she plans on stopping by and making a donation. She then stated this will be an interesting election year, and sometimes people take sides and they don't talk to their neighbors and she is going to focus on 4 legislatures that are reaching across the Isle and are working together so we can see that people with different political backgrounds can work together and pass laws that are beneficial for the people of South Lyon and she has begun her research project, and she wants everyone to encourage everyone to work together despite different positions.

Councilmember Dilg thanked the Girl Scouts for coming and learning about local government. She then thanked Jeff Heinanen and his team for his amazing event on New Years Eve and it was a great event, it looked great and she appreciated everyone being able to enjoy the ball drop even if they weren't in the barn. It looked great and it was fun.

Councilmember Hansen stated he brought letters that he drafted to elected officials to invite everyone to the 20th Annual Creek Clean Up. (Please see attached letter). It is April 21th from 9am to 1pm. This will help protect our creeks and streams by safely removing trash and debris within the Huron Valley Watershed. He stated volunteers are encouraged to wear bright yellow shirts and long pants, rubber boots and gloves, and meet at 400 McMunn at the Michigan Seamless Tube parking lot. The event will begin with a short lecture and instructions, followed by groups of volunteers being assigned to different areas of the city. Volunteers will have a chance to ask questions and following the event, pizza and refreshments will be provided for all volunteers. He then stated to RSVP or ask questions, contact Suzann Martin at 248-437-4942. This is similar to the letter we sent last year, and we are hoping to have a better turnout than last year.

Councilmember Kivell stated DPW did a great job during a really bad environment and they did it with smiles and he commends them.

<a id="p13"></a>
*[Page 13 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/2-12-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202402090926490#page=13)* (read by OCR; check the original)

Councilmember Mosier stated the DPW did an outstanding job considering the weather they had to work with. She then reminded everyone of the Farmers Market this weekend at the VFW. She then stated the Ropers Barn Euchre Tournament fundraising event is Friday and she hasn't played in years, but she may give it a shot. She then stated the VFW's pancake breakfast that is coming up in February. She then stated she wanted to send her condolences to the Veltri family. Joe Veltri was our code and building inspector for many years and he passed away last Thursday and the family is in our thoughts and prayers.

Councilmember Bogert stated on December 13 $ ^{th} $ the Youth Assistance hosted its annual shop with a hero event and 90 young people had the opportunity to shop with first responders and he thanked Chief Baaki and Chief Thorington for providing staff and also attending the event. It was a wonderful event and it was nice seeing the Christmas spirit in action.

Mayor Kennedy stated he wanted to recognize the City's DPW workers for their Herculean efforts last week addressing the snow and ice throughout the city. They spent long days working to ensure the streets were clear so residents could get to where they needed to go and to do so safely. Many of them stayed overnight and did not go home in case they couldn't make it back the next day and others came back in in the middle of the night to continue clearing the areas throughout the city. He then stated you will not find more dedicated, hard-working folks than those in the City of South Lyon DPW and he along with all of the other residents, just want to say, thank you so very much for everything.

ADJOURNMENT

CM 12-11-23 MOTION TO ADJOURN

Motion by Kurtzweil, supported by Hansen

Motion to adjourn at 9:46 p.m.

VOTE: MOTION CARRIED UNANIMOUSLY

Respectfully submitted,

Mayor Steve Kennedy

# AGENDA NOTE Consent Agenda: Item #1

MEETING DATE: February 12, 2024

PERSON PLACING ITEM ON AGENDA: City Clerk

AGENDA TOPIC: Revised Resolution for granting poverty exemption from payment of taxes

EXPLANATION OF TOPIC: In August 2022, Council approved an updated resolution for granting poverty exemption from payment of taxes. Our Assessor and the Board of Review Members have requested Council to approve a new resolution based upon the State Tax Commission now allowing 75% reduction in taxes. We have updated item #12 in the resolution originally passed with the language “The Board of Review may award full and/or partial exemptions as permitted by law.”

MATERIALS ATTACHED AS SUPPORTING DOCUMENTS: Resolution 01-24 reflecting the new language, “The Board of Review may award full and/or partial exemptions as permitted by law”.

POSSIBLE COURSES OF ACTION: To adopt or not adopt the Resolution 01-24

SUGGESTED MOTION: Moved by, _____ seconded by, _____ to adopt Resolution 01-24 establishing polices and guidelines for granting poverty exemption from payment of property taxes.

<a id="p34"></a>
*[Page 34 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/2-12-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202402090926490#page=34)* (read by OCR; check the original)

# RESOLUTION NO. 01-24 CITY OF SOUTH LYON OAKLAND COUNTY, MICHIGAN

## RESOLUTION ESTABLISHING POLICIES AND GUIDELINES FOR GRANTING POVERTY EXEMPTION FROM PAYMENT OF PROPERTY TAXES

WHEREAS, the adoption of guidelines for poverty exemptions is required of the City Council; and

WHEREAS, the principal residence of person, who the Board of Review determines by reason of poverty to be unable to contribute to the public charge, is eligible for exemption in whole or in part from taxation under Public Act 253 of 2020 (MCL.211.7u): and

WHEREAS, pursuant to Public Act 253 of 2020, the City of South Lyon, Oakland County, adopts the following policies and guidelines for the Board of Review to implement. The policies and guidelines shall be applied to the information provided in a Michigan Department of Treasury Form 5737, application for MCL.211.7u poverty exemption, which application includes, but is not limited to, the disclosure of the specific income and asset levels of the claimant and all persons residing in the household, including any property tax credit returns filed in the current or immediately preceding year;

AND WHEREAS, P.A 253 of 2020 also amended section 7u of Act No. 206 of Public Act of 1893, as amended by Act. No. 313 of the Public Acts of 1993, being section 211.7u of the Michigan Compiled Laws and required the use of state prescribed forms and limited to certain percentages the exemption amount which could be granted.

WHEREAS, to be eligible, a person shall do all the following on an annual basis:

1. Be the “sole” owner(s) of and occupy, as a principal residence, the property for which an exemption is requested

2. The subject property must be classified as an “improved single family residential” or “residential condominium” property with a valid Homeowner’s Principal Residence Exemption currently in effect.

3. File the Michigan Department of Treasury Form 5737, application for MCL.211.7u Poverty Exemption with the Board of Review by returning it to the City Assessor's office, accompanied by all documents required by the form including Form 5739, Affirmation of Ownership and Occupancy to Remain Exempt by Reason of Poverty. If the applicant or the other household members have not filed tax returns, a Form 4988- Poverty Exemption Affidavit and a statement from the Social Security Administration and/or Michigan Social Services as to monies paid to applicant(s) during the previous year must be completed and submitted. Disabled applicants may call the Assessor's office to make arrangements for assistance with filing the application.

<a id="p35"></a>
*[Page 35 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/2-12-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202402090926490#page=35)* (read by OCR; check the original)

4. File an application reporting that the assets of all persons in the household do not exceed two times the federal poverty income guidelines amount for the affective household size. Assets generally include: all other real estate, motor vehicles, recreational vehicles and equipment, insurance, retirement funds, trust assets, checking accounts, savings accounts, certificates of deposit, cash, stocks, bonds, time share units, artworks, antiques, coins, precious metals or stones, jewelry, guns, equipment, tax refunds, gifts, loans, other investments, or personal property value.

5. Applicants must meet the federal poverty income guidelines as defined and determined annually by the United States Department of Health and Human Services established by the State Tax Commission to be updated annually. All applicants must own and occupy the property as a homestead on the property for which the exemption is being requested. All applicants may be asked to verify ownership of the property and provide personal identification upon request of the Board of Review.

6. Produce a valid driver's license or other form of identification, if requested.

7. Produce, if requested, a deed, land contract, or other evidence of ownership of the property for which an exemption is requested.

8. All property taxes must be paid and current.

9. The application for an exemption shall be filed after January 1, and at least one day prior to the last day of the Board of Review. The filing of the application constitutes an appearance before the Board of Review for the purpose of preserving the right of appeal to the Michigan Tax Tribunal.

10. Applicants need not appear before the Board of Review, but are encouraged to do so to be available to provide further information or clarification to the Board of Review. Applicants who do not initially appear may be required to appear at the Board of Review to respond to questions. Applicants should understand that the failure to appear at the Board of Review, if requested by the Board of Review may result in denial of the application. If the applicant is disabled, alternate procedures may be allowed pursuant to the city's obligation in the application of the Americans with Disabilities Act.

11. The Board of Review shall in no instance require an applicant to have less household income than the applicable poverty income level for the household.

12. The Board of Review may award full and/or partial exemptions as permitted under law

WHEREAS, the federal poverty income guidelines are updated annually by the United States Department of Health and Human Services, and the annual allowable income guidelines include income for all persons residing in the principal residence.

NOW, THEREOFRE, BE IT HEREBY RESOLVED that the Board of Review shall follow the above stated policies guidelines in granting or denying an exemption. The same standards shall apply to each taxpayer within the city claiming the poverty exemption for the assessment year.

<a id="p36"></a>
*[Page 36 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/2-12-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202402090926490#page=36)* (read by OCR; check the original)

BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that in order to ease the burden of taxpayers, the assessor and the Board of Review and to ensure that all taxpayers have an equal opportunity to be heard by the Board of Review, the City of South Lyon hereby resolves, according to provisions of MCL.211.30(8) of the General Property Tax Act, that the Board of Review shall receive letters of protest regarding assessments from resident taxpayers from the first Tuesday in March until it adjourns from the public hearings for which it meets to hear such protests. All notice of assessment change and all advertisements of the Board of Review meetings are to include a statement that the resident taxpayers may protest by letter to the Board, be it further resolved that this resolution is hereby given immediate effect and will stay in effect for subsequent years until amended or voided.

# AGENDA NOTE Consent Agenda #2

MEETING DATE: February 12, 2024

PERSON PLACING ITEM ON AGENDA: Joey Thorington, Fire Chief

AGENDA TOPIC: Mutual Aid Agreement between the City of South Lyon and Green Oak Charter Township

EXPLANATION OF TOPIC: The Washtenaw Area Mutual Aid Association (WAMAA) raised their annual membership dues. Because WAMAA emergency services/rescue teams provided duplicate services that Oakland County also provides, it was decided not to re-new the SLFD membership at the end of the last calendar year. Automatic mutual aid services that were previously covered between The City of South Lyon Fire and Green Oak Charter Township Fire Departments through WAMAA now need a new standalone agreement. This new agreement does not change the way the two fire departments have been working together.

MATERIALS ATTACHED AS SUPPORTING DOCUMENTS: Mutual Aid Agreement between The City of South Lyon and Green Oak Township

POSSIBLE COURSES OF ACTION: Approve/not approve the Mutual Aid Agreement between The City of South Lyon and Green Oak Charter Township.

SUGGESTED MOTION: Motion by ___, supported by ___, to approve the Mutual Aid Agreement between The City of South Lyon and Green Oak Charter Township.

*[Page 38 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/2-12-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202402090926490#page=38)* (read by OCR; check the original)

# MUTUAL AID AGREEMENT FOR FIRE PROTECTION AND RESCUE SERVICES between

## GREEN OAK CHARTER TOWNSHIP AND THE CITY OF SOUTH LYON, MICHIGAN

This Mutual Aid Agreement (“Agreement”) is entered into between GREEN OAK CHARTER TOWNSHIP, a Michigan municipal corporation, whose address is 10001 Silver Lake Road, Brighton, MI 48116 (the “Township”), and the CITY OF SOUTH LYON, a Michigan municipal corporation, whose address is 335 S. Warren Street, South Lyon, MI 48178 (the “City”).

This Mutual Aid Agreement for fire protection and emergency services as set forth herein is entered into pursuant to and under the authority of the Urban Cooperation Act of 1967, Act 7 of the Public Acts of 1967, as amended, and the Intergovernmental Contracts Between Municipal Corporations Act, Act 35 of the Public Acts of 1951, as amended.

## THE PARTIES AGREE AS FOLLOWS:

## ARTICLE I – PURPOSE

The purpose of this Agreement is for the two communities to provide certain mutual aid services to each other as needed in portions of their respective communities to afford and promote more effective fire and rescue services within the Township and the City. The primary goal of this Agreement is to improve the life safety of the citizenry by providing the quickest response of apparatus to an emergency, by supplying seamless service through the elimination of artificial service boundaries.

## ARTICLE II – CRITERIA

Whenever possible, the closest personnel and pieces of fire and/or emergency apparatus will be dispatched. This entails the entire City and the eastern areas of Township jurisdictions.

Generally, this dispatch will occur under the following situations:

- The Township will be dispatched through Livingston County Central Dispatch to structure fires within the City, and the City will be dispatched to structure fires in the eastern areas of the Township.

• Emergency medical incidents upon request of the municipality with jurisdiction as needed.

- In the event of a major disaster or other emergency, either of the parties may need the assistance of the other party to this Agreement to provide supplemental fire suppression and/or emergency medical service equipment and/or personnel, but only if the party whose assistance is requested have such resources necessary to provide such assistance available.

## ARTICLE III - REIMBURSEMENT

Neither the City nor the Township will receive payment or be reimbursed by the other party for any expenses incurred in connection with the services provided under this Agreement. Nothing herein prevents either party from receiving reimbursements from FEMA or any other state or federal reimbursement programs.

<a id="p39"></a>
*[Page 39 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/2-12-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202402090926490#page=39)* (read by OCR; check the original)

# ARTICLE IV – INSURANCE

Each Party agrees to maintain adequate insurance or self-insurance for any liability, claim, loss, damages, demands, costs or judgments, including attorney fees, for bodily injury, including death or property damage arising out of or related to the acts or omissions of its employees and agents to the extent provided by law.

## ARTICLE V – LIABILITY

Each Party will be solely responsible for the acts or omissions of their respective employees and agents and for the costs associated with those acts and the defenses of those acts. The Parties shall not be responsible for any liability or costs associated with those acts and the defense of those acts for Parties who are not their employees or agents. Each Party shall assume liability for damages to its own equipment or apparatus.

## ARTICLE VI – EFFECTIVE DATE

This Agreement is effective on the date it is signed by both parties, who may sign in separate counterparts.

## ARTICLE VII – TERM

This Agreement shall remain in effect unless or until either party cancels this Agreement by giving a minimum of ninety (90) days written notice. Scheduled periodic reviews will remain in place for the life of this Agreement to assure quality assurance and integrity.

## ARTICLE VIII - MISCELLANEOUS PROVISIONS

8.1 No waiver of Governmental Immunity. The Parties agree that no provision of this Agreement is intended, nor shall be construed, as a waiver by any Party of any governmental immunity as provided by the Urban Cooperation Act or otherwise under law.

8.2 No Third-Party Beneficiaries. This Agreement does not confer any rights or remedies on any third party, other than the Parties to this Agreement.

8.3 Governing Law. This Agreement is made and entered into in the State of Michigan and shall in all respects be interpreted, enforced and governed under the laws of the State of Michigan.

## Parade/Event Insurance Requirements

The applicant/organization must provide a separate hold harmless agreement and certificate of insurance and documentation of the following at the time of application.

1.) Commercial General Liability Insurance: The applicant shall procure and maintain during the life of this permit, commercial general liability insurance on an occurrence basis with limits of liability not less than $1,000,000 per occurrence and aggregate.

2.) Motor Vehicle Liability: The applicant shall procure and maintain during the life of this permit, Motor Vehicle Liability Insurance including Michigan no-no fault coverages, with limits of liability not than $1,000,000 per occurrence combined single limit for bodily injury and property damage. Coverage shall include all owner vehicles, all non-owned vehicles and all hired vehicles.

3.) Liquor Liability: If alcohol beverages will be served, the applicant shall provide proof of liquor Liability coverage with limit not less than $1,000,000 per occurrence and aggregate; name the City of South Lyon as additional insured.

4.) Additional Insured: Commercial General Liability, Motor Vehicle Liability and Liquor Liability as described above shall include an endorsement stating that the following shall be additional insured; The City of South Lyon, all elected and appointed officials, all employees, volunteers, all boards, commissions, authorities, and board members. It is understood and agreed by naming the City of South Lyon as additional insured, coverage afforded is considered to be primary and any other insurance the City of South Lyon may have in effect shall be considered secondary and/or excess.

5.) Cancellation Notice: All Liability Insurances as described above, shall include an endorsement stating, "It is understood and agreed that 30 days, 10 days for non-payment of premium, advance written notice of cancellation, non-renewal, reduction and/or material change shall be sent to City Manager, Paul Zelenak 335 S. Warren, South Lyon, MI 48178.

6.) Proof of Insurance Coverage: The contractor or its subcontractor's shall provide the city of South Lyon at the time the contracts are returned for execution, the certificates and policies as listed. 1.) One copy of Certificate of Insurance for Workers Compensation. 2.) One copy of Certificate of Insurance for Commercial General Liability. 3.) One copy of Certificate of Insurance for Vehicle Liability. 4.) Original policy or original binder pending issuance of policy for Owners and Contractor Protective Liability Insurance. 5.) Certified copies of all policies mentioned above will be furnished if requested.

7.) If any of the above coverages expire during the term of this contract, the contractor or its subcontractors shall deliver renewal certificates and/or policies to City of South Lyon at least 10 days prior to the expiration date.

# AGENDA NOTE Unfinished Business Item #1

MEETING DATE: February 12, 2024

PERSON PLACING ITEM ON AGENDA: Recommendation from Planning Commission on November 16, 2023

AGENDA TOPIC: First Reading of Ordinance to Amend the Official Zoning Map of the City of South Lyon by conditionally rezoning portions of the total 34.17+/- acre parcel on the east side of Dixboro Road, south of Ten Mile/Lake Street, parcel # 21-30-126-003, zoned I-1, Light Industrial, as follows: 24.39+/- acres of RM-3, Multiple-Family Residential and 1.63+/- acres R-3, Single-Family Residential. The remaining 8.14+/- acres will remain as I-1, Light Industrial.

EXPLANATION OF TOPIC: Elevate Land Holdings, LLC has petitioned the City and requested a Conditional Rezoning of a portion of a 34.17+/- acre parcel on the east side of Dixboro Road, south of Ten Mile/Lake Street, parcel #21-30-126-003, to allow 252 new multiple-family residential units, 3 single-family residential lots, and the remaining portion of the parcel to be developed with approximately 64,000 square feet of industrial development. The petitioner's request is pursuant to MCL 125.3405, which authorizes rezoning with conditions voluntarily offered by the owner of the property. The Planning Commission held a public hearing for the request on November 16, 2023 and recommended approval of the request for the conditional rezoning based on the conditions voluntarily offered in writing by the owner.

The ordinance and preliminary site plan were before City Council at the January 22, 2024 meeting, where there was much discussion regarding the proposed re-location of the entrance drive off of Ten Mile and the potential impacts to the wetlands. The City Council tabled the item to allow the applicant to revise the offered conditions and provide an updated site plan, keeping the entrance drive in its existing location. The conditions voluntarily offered by the applicant have been updated in a revised draft of the conditional rezoning agreement. It should be noted that the phasing schedule has shifted slightly with the modification, including construction of the clubhouse and some amenities with Phase 1 rather than during Phase 2.

# ORDINANCE NO. ___ CITY OF SOUTH LYON OAKLAND COUNTY, MICHIGAN

# AN ORDINANCE TO AMEND THE OFFICIAL ZONING MAP OF THE CITY OF SOUTH LYON CONDITIONALLY REZONING A PORTION OF PARCEL NO. 21-30-126-003, VACANT PROPERTY, FROM THE I-1 DISTRICT (LIGHT INDUSTRIAL) TO THE RM-3 DISTRICT (MULTIPLE FAMILY RESIDENTIAL) AND R-3 DISTRICT (SINGLE FAMILY RESIDENTIAL)

THE CITY OF SOUTH LYON ORDAINS:

PART I. Amendment of Official Zoning Map. The Official Zoning Map of the City of South Lyon incorporated into the South Lyon Zoning Ordinance by Section 102-182 is hereby amended to conditionally rezone a portion of the vacant Property located on the east side of Dixboro Road, south of Ten Mile/Lake Street, Tax ID No. 21-30-126-003 and more fully described as:

WHEREAS, the Property is currently zoned I-1 (Light Industrial) and Owner desires to develop a mixed-use development to include a Multiple Family Residential and a Single Family Residential development project on a portion of the Property which is not permitted under the Property's I-1 (Light Industrial) zoning district, and to develop a portion of the Property in conformance with the Property's existing I-1 Light Industrial zoning classification;

WHEREAS, pursuant to Section 405 of the Michigan Zoning Enabling Act, 2008 PA 110, MCL 125.3405 et seq., as amended, certain conditions voluntarily offered by the Owner of the Property, may become a condition of rezoning the Property;

WHEREAS, on or about June 6, 2023, a representative of the Owner, submitted a Conditional Rezoning Application for the Property along with a preliminary site plan, a written supplement, preliminary architectural drawings, and a preliminary landscape plan, voluntarily offering, in writing, certain conditions to rezone the Property to PUD (Planned Unit Development);

WHEREAS, on September 28, 2023, the City Planning Commission held a public hearing, after publication of notice thereof, on the Owner's Conditional Rezoning Application;

WHEREAS, on September 28, 2023, the City Planning Commission recommended approval of the Owner's request for conditional rezoning based upon the conditions voluntarily offered by Owner in writing;

WHEREAS, after receiving a favorable recommendation from the Planning Commission, but before the recommendation was presented to City Council, the Owner, City Planner, City Attorney and City Manager reevaluated the Owner's request to conditionally rezone to PUD (Planned Unit Development) and agreed that the conditional rezoning should have been to RM-3 (Multiple Family Residential) and R-3 (Single Family Residential) for a portion of the Property.

*[Page 58 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/2-12-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202402090926490#page=58)* (read by OCR; check the original)

WHEREAS, on November 16, 2023, the City Planning Commission held a public hearing, after publication of notice thereof, on the Owner's Updated Conditional Rezoning Application;

WHEREAS, on November 16, 2023, the City Planning Commission recommended approval of the Owner's request for conditional rezoning based upon the conditions voluntarily offered by Owner in writing which are set forth in this Agreement and the attached Exhibit A;

WHEREAS, on _____ 2024, the City Council approved the request for Conditional Rezoning and adopted Ordinance No. _____ based upon the conditions voluntarily offered by the Owner in writing which are set forth in this Agreement and the attached Exhibit A; and

NOW, THEREFORE, in consideration of the foregoing and the mutual promises hereinafter set forth, the City and Owner agree as follows:

1.  $ \underline{\text{Conditional Rezoning}} $. Pursuant to Section 405 of the Michigan Zoning Enabling Act, MCL 125.3405, Owner agrees to limit the development and use of the Property as set forth in the rezoning conditions below as a condition of rezoning. Upon execution of this Agreement by the parties, the City shall effectuate the conditional rezoning of the Residential Property from I-1 District (Limited Industrial) to the RM-3 District (Multiple Family Residential) and R-3 District (Single Family Residential), as legally described herein, on the City's official Zoning Map. This Agreement is valid and entered into on a voluntary basis and represents a permissible exercise of authority by the City pursuant to MCL 125.3405.

2.  $ \underline{\text{Rezoning Conditions}} $. Owner agrees to develop and use the Property in accordance with the following conditions which have been offered voluntarily in writing by the Owner as conditions to the rezoning:

a. Development and use of the Property shall be for a phased development of Multiple Family Residential and Single Family Residential on the Residential Property, and a phased development of Industrial buildings on the remaining portion of the Property zoned I-1 District (Light Industrial) in substantial conformance with the preliminary

<a id="p59"></a>
*[Page 59 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/2-12-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202402090926490#page=59)* (read by OCR; check the original)

site plan, attached as Exhibit B, and in accordance with the Time Limits described in Paragraph 6, below, consisting of:

(1) 24.40\pm/ - acres of Multiple Family Residential Property, 1.63\pm/ - acres of Single-Family Residential Property and 8.14\pm/ - acres of Light Industrial Property;

(2) 252 Multiple Family 1-, 2-, and 3-bedroom residential units on the Property rezoned RM-3 District (Multiple Family Residential);

(3) 3 single family residential detached dwelling units on the Property rezoned R-3 District (Single Family Residential); and

(4) Approximately 64,000 square feet of industrial building space on the I-1 District (Light Industrial).

(collectively referred to as the "Project"). The Project will be developed in the phases as depicted on the preliminary site plan attached as Exhibit B and as follows: Phase 1 of the Industrial development, shall include one 19,840 +/- sq. ft. industrial building; Phase 1 of the Multiple Family Residential shall include Buildings 4-9, 84 units total plus the clubhouse and certain amenities; Phase 2 of the Multiple Family Residential shall include Buildings 10-13, 84 units total Phase 3 of the Multiple Family Residential shall include Buildings 14-18, 84 units total. The future development of the remaining I-1 (Light Industrial) zoned property and the R-3 (Single Family Residential) zoned property will be developed in accordance with the underlying zoning district.

b. Development of Phase 1 of the Industrial development and Phase 1 of the Multiple Family Residential development to occur concurrently.

c. Constructed as part of Phase 1 of the Industrial development and Phase 1 of the Multiple Family Residential development, the paving of 1,088 lineal feet of Dixboro Road from Lake Street to the southerly end of the Property.

d. At the time of Dixboro Road paving, the pedestrian pathway ultimately connecting to Huron Valley Trail, measuring 1,088 lineal feet along Dixboro Rd, will be installed from Lake Street to the southerly end of the Property line;

e. As part of Phase 1 of the Industrial development and Phase 1 of the Multiple Family Residential Development, a payment of $36,200 to the City of South Lyon will be made for the future construction of the remaining 724 lineal feet from the southerly Property line and Huron Valley Trail.

As a part of Phase 2 of the Multiple Family Residential development, a pedestrian pathway (connecting to the pedestrian pathway constructed in Phase 1) will be constructed along Lake Street from the corner of Lake Street and Dixboro Road to South Hagadorn Road connecting to the existing sidewalk that leads to downtown.

g. Owner shall secure and record all necessary shared access, use, utility, facilities, parking and other easements between the Project and adjacent development Westlake Place Apartments (Parcel ID 21-30-126-005) as required by and in a form approved by the City.

<a id="p60"></a>
*[Page 60 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/2-12-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202402090926490#page=60)* (read by OCR; check the original)

Minimum floor area reduction to allow for 1-bedroom units to be a minimum of 600 sq. ft., 2-bedroom units to be a minimum of 768 sq. ft. and 3-bedroom units to be a minimum of 936 sq. ft. to meet market demand for 1 - 3 bedroom units and to allow preservation of nine (9) acres of wetlands and 50% of the existing woodlands.

i. 572 parking spaces (which includes parking spaces within the existing Westlake Place Apartments, (Parcel ID 21-30-126-005), which will be available for use within the Project) provided for use by the Multiple Family Residential Property as recommended in the traffic study conducted by Fleis and Vandenbrink, which reduction will reduce impervious surfaces, increase available open space and area for community garden, recreation courts and a dog park.

j. 223 parking spaces provided for the industrial use to be located on the portion of the Property within the I-1 (Light Industrial) zoning district. 67 parking spaces shall be provided for the Phase 1 Industrial development and the additional parking spaces are proposed because tenants and/or users have not been identified or secured for the Industrial use. Once a site plan is submitted for the specific configuration of the remaining Light Industrial district, the City shall determine the required number of parking spaces required or shall cause the Owner to conduct a parking and/or traffic study to establish a basis for the City to consider the appropriate number of parking spaces required. If an amendment to the final approved site plan is required, it shall be considered a minor revision.

k. For the efficient use of the Property, considering the Project meets several master plan goals, the buffering requirement of the City's Zoning Ordinance between industrial and residential uses must be waived and, once tenants are secured and a site plan submitted, the City shall determine the appropriate buffer requirement considering the proposed industrial use.

3.  $ \underline{\text{Use of the Property}} $. Development and use of the Property shall be in substantial conformance with the approved preliminary site plan, attached as Exhibit B, and shall be subject to and in conformance with the approval of the final site plan for the Property by the City Planning Commission. The parties agree that minor revisions to the approved final site plan which, in the sole discretion of the City Manager, do not significantly modify the proposed development or use of the Property or the site plan, may be considered and approved by the City administratively without the necessity of review and approval by the Planning Commission and/or without amendment of this Agreement and exhibits hereto.

4.  $ \underline{\text{Compliance with Laws}} $. Except as modified herein, development and use of the Property shall comply with all other applicable state, county and local requirements for land

<a id="p61"></a>
*[Page 61 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/2-12-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202402090926490#page=61)* (read by OCR; check the original)

development and use, ordinances, codes, standards, regulations, requirements, and engineering design standards and requirements.

5.  $ \underline{\text{Compliance with Conditions}} $. Once commenced and established, the development and use of the Property must remain in compliance with the conditions of the conditional rezoning Final Site Plan and as provided in this Agreement. Any failure to comply with a condition contained herein shall constitute a violation of the City of South Lyon Zoning Ordinance and shall be punishable as provided for therein. Additionally, any such violation shall be deemed a nuisance per se and subject to abatement as provided for by law.

6.  $ \underline{\text{Time Limits}} $. The City and Owner agree that the following approvals and activities are conditions to this Agreement and if not completed within the following time limits, the Residential Property may revert back to the I-1 (Light Industrial) zoning classification:

a. Final site plan approval within twelve (12) months from the date of this Agreement;

b. A building permit for the Phase 1 Multiple Family Residential and the Phase 1 Industrial development thereof, if applicable, is obtained within one (1) year from the date of final site plan approval;

c. A building permit for Phase 2 of the Multiple Family Residential is obtained within twenty-four (24) months of the issuance of the first building permit for Phase 1 of the Multiple Family Residential.

d. A building permit for Phase 3 of the Multiple-Family Residential is obtained within twenty-four (24) months of the issuance of the first building permit for Phase 2 of the Multiple Family Residential.

e. A final certificate of occupancy for the entire Multiple Family Residential, or Phase 2, or Phase 3 thereof if applicable, is obtained within thirty (30) months from the date the building permit was issued for said Phase.

7.  $ \underline{\text{Extension of Time Limits}} $. The City, in the City's exclusive discretion, may agree to extend the time limits in Paragraph 6 upon receipt of a written request from the Owner but the City shall not be required to grant such request for extension. Any extension granted shall be in writing signed by both parties.

<a id="p62"></a>
*[Page 62 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/2-12-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202402090926490#page=62)* (read by OCR; check the original)

8.  $ \underline{\text{City Right to Rezone}} $. In the event that the time limit conditions in Paragraph 6 are not met, nor extended upon mutual agreement of the parties pursuant to Paragraph 7; nothing in this Agreement shall be deemed to prohibit the City from rezoning all or any portion of the Property which is subject to this Agreement to another zoning district. Any rezoning shall be conducted in compliance with the City's Zoning Ordinance and the Michigan Zoning Enabling Act.

9. Owner Acknowledgments. Owner acknowledges that it voluntarily offered the conditions contained in this Agreement, including all Exhibits, in connection with the request for conditional rezoning of the Property and that such conditions were offered in writing. Owner acknowledges that upon the approval of the requested conditional rezoning and this Agreement becoming effective, the development and use of the Property shall conform thereafter with the terms and conditions of the conditional rezoning, this Agreement, and all the requirements regulating development and use within the zoning district as modified by the voluntarily offered conditions and with this Agreement and that any failure to fully comply with such conditions and/or this Agreement shall constitute a violation of the City of South Lyon Zoning Ordinance, and shall be punishable accordingly. Owner acknowledges and agrees that the terms and conditions of this Agreement are necessary and roughly proportional to the burden imposed by the conditional rezoning of the Property, and are therefore necessary to ensure that public services and facilities will be capable of accommodating the development and use and the increased service of facility loads caused by the development and use, to protect the natural environment and conserve natural resources, to ensure compatibility with adjacent uses of land, and to promote the use of the Property in a socially and economically feasible manner, and to achieve other legitimate objectives authorized by law. Owner agrees that the terms and conditions herein do not constitute a taking of property for any purpose or a violation of any

<a id="p63"></a>
*[Page 63 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/2-12-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202402090926490#page=63)* (read by OCR; check the original)

Constitutional right, and Owner agrees to be bound by each and every provision and conditions of this Agreement. Owner acknowledges and agrees that if the conditional rezoning becomes void for any reason, then no further development shall take place and no permits shall be issued unless and until a new zoning district classification for the Property has been established.

10.  $ \underline{\text{Entire Agreement}} $. This Agreement and Exhibits, together with any agreements referenced herein, constitutes the entire agreement between the parties with respect to the subject of this Agreement.

11.  $ \underline{\text{Binding Effect, Running with the Land}} $. This Agreement shall be binding on and inure to the benefit of the parties and their respective successors, heirs, assigns and transferees, and shall run with the land.

12.  $ \underline{\text{Amendments}} $. The terms of this Agreement may be amended, changed, or modified, but only by written agreement executed by the required parties. Any amendment of this Agreement shall be recorded in the Oakland County Register of Deeds.

13.  $ \underline{\text{Governing Law}} $. This Agreement shall be governed by, construed, and enforced in accordance with Michigan law.

14.  $ \underline{\text{Authority to Execute}} $. The parties each represent and state that the individuals signing this Agreement are fully authorized to execute this document and bind their respective parties to the terms and conditions contained herein.

15.  $ \underline{\text{Enforcement}} $. In the event of a proceeding to enforce this Agreement, a prevailing party may seek to recover its costs and attorney fees in addition to any other applicable and available relief.

16.  $ \underline{\text{Joint Drafting}} $. No provision of this Agreement shall be construed against or interpreted to the disadvantage of one party against any other party by any court or other

<a id="p64"></a>
*[Page 64 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/2-12-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202402090926490#page=64)* (read by OCR; check the original)

governmental authority by reason of any determination or assertion that one party was chiefly or primarily responsible for having drafted this Agreement.

17.  $ \underline{\text{Conflicts}} $. In the event of conflict between the provisions of this Agreement and the provisions of another applicable ordinance, code, regulations, requirement, standard, or policy, the provisions of this Agreement shall prevail.

18.  $ \underline{\text{Severability}} $. This invalidity of any provision of this Agreement shall not affect the validity of the remaining provisions, which shall remain valid and enforceable to the fullest extent permitted by law.

19.  $ \underline{\text{Recording}} $. This Agreement shall be binding on the parties and the Property and shall be recorded with the Oakland County Register of Deeds.

20.  $ \underline{\text{Counterparts}} $. This Agreement and any amendments to it may be executed in multiple counterparts, each of which shall be deemed an original and all of which shall constitute one Agreement. The signature of any party to any counterpart shall be deemed to be a signature to, and may be appended to, any other counterpart.

IN WITNESS WHEREOF, the parties hereto have executed this Agreement on the date first set forth above.

PART II. Severability. Should any section, subdivision, clause, or phrase of this Ordinance be declared by the courts to be invalid, the validity of the Ordinance as a whole, or in part, shall not be affected other than the part invalidated.

PART III. Savings Clause. This Ordinance amends the Zoning Ordinance only as specified herein, and the Zoning Ordinance shall remain in full force and effect.

PART IV. Repealer. All other Ordinances or parts of Ordinances in conflict herewith are hereby repealed only to the extent necessary to give this Ordinance full force and effect.

PART V. Publication. The City Clerk shall publish this Ordinance in the manner required by law and shall publish at the same time, a notice of the adoption of this Ordinance and stating that a copy of the Ordinance is available to the public at the office of the City Clerk for inspection.

PART VI. ___ Effective Date. This Ordinance shall be effective on the date provided by applicable law following publication.
