*[Page 210 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/2-12-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202402090926490#page=210)* (read by OCR; check the original)

# AGREEMENT REGARDING CONVEYANCE AND DEVELOPMENT OF PROPERTY

THIS AGREEMENT (“Agreement”), made and entered into this ___ day of ___, 2024 (the “Effective Date”), by and between the CITY OF SOUTH LYON (“Seller”), a Michigan Municipal Corporation, whose address is 335 S. Warren Street, South Lyon, Michigan 48178, and LIVONIA BUILDERS, G2, LLC, a limited liability company whose address is 18261 Shelley Pond, Northville, Michigan 48168 (“Purchaser”).

## IN CONSIDERATION OF THE MUTUAL COVENANTS CONTAINED HEREIN, PURCHASER AND SELLER AGREE AS FOLLOWS:

Purchaser hereby offers and agrees to purchase from the Seller, and the Seller agrees to sell to the Purchaser, the property commonly known as 318 W. Lake Street, South Lyon, Michigan, Tax ID #80-21-19-456-026, legally described Lot 3, ARM'S SUBDIVISION, according to the plat thereof recorded in Liber 16, Page 26, Oakland County Records, and any rights or easements presently existing for the benefit therefore (the "Property") subject to the terms and conditions herein set forth.

1.  $ \underline{\text{Purchase Price}} $. Purchaser shall pay to the Seller Seventy Thousand Dollars (70,000.00), which amount shall be referred to in this Agreement as the "Purchase Price." Payment of the Purchase Price shall be made in cash, bank cashier's check or wire transfer of funds to an account designated in writing by Seller and shall be delivered at Closing subject to the terms and conditions stated in this Agreement.

2.  $ \underline{\text{Title}} $. Purchaser shall order a commitment for an owner's policy of title insurance (the "Title Commitment") in the amount of the Purchase Price within ten (10) calendar days of the date of this Agreement and shall deliver a copy of the Title Commitment to Seller. Purchaser shall be responsible for obtaining a final policy of title insurance. Purchaser agrees to pay for the Title Commitment and final policy of title insurance.

Purchaser shall have ten (10) calendar days after the date on which Purchaser receives the title commitment to review same. If Purchaser determines that any lien, claim, easement, restriction, encumbrance, encroachment, lease or right of any party may interfere with Purchaser's contemplated use of the Property or is otherwise unacceptable to Purchaser for any reason whatsoever (hereinafter "Title Defect"), Purchaser shall notify Seller of any such Title Defect within five (5) calendar days after said ten (10) day period. Any exception set forth in the commitment to which Purchaser does not timely object shall be waived. Seller shall use its best efforts to cure any Title Defect of which it is timely notified. If Seller fails to cure all such Title Defects within thirty (30) calendar days after the receipt of Purchaser's timely notice thereof, Purchaser's sole remedies are to either (i) terminate this Agreement, or (ii) waive any such Title Defect(s) and proceed to closing accepting title "AS IS." Seller's failure to correct a title defect shall not be deemed a default under this Agreement.

<a id="p211"></a>
*[Page 211 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/2-12-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202402090926490#page=211)* (read by OCR; check the original)

3.  $ \underline{\text{Taxes and Prorated Items}} $. Any taxes and assessments which have become a lien upon the Property as of the date of closing shall be paid by Seller. Purchaser shall be responsible for the payment of all property taxes falling due after the date of closing without regard to lien date.

4. Prior to Conveyance to Purchaser. Prior to the Seller executing and delivering a warranty deed (in the form attached hereto as Exhibit "A") conveying the Property to Purchaser, Purchaser shall have completed the following: (a) make application, pay all applicable fees, and obtain all required permits from the Seller and any other governmental entity having jurisdiction for the commencement of construction of a single-family residence on the Property in compliance with all applicable codes, ordinances, laws and regulations; (b) pay the Purchase Price to the Seller with verification to Seller that such payment has cleared with funds deposited into the Seller's desired bank account; (c) pay to Seller all required transfer taxes and costs associated with recording the deed from the Seller; and (d) execute a Property Transfer Affidavit as required by law and any and all other required documents required for the conveyance of the Property. No construction shall take place on the Property until the Property has been conveyed to Purchaser.

5.  $ \underline{\text{Upon Conveyance to Purchase}} $. Seller shall deliver to Purchaser, and Purchaser shall accept Seller's possession of the Property as of the date and time of the Seller's execution and delivery of the deed conveying the Property to Purchaser.

6.  $ \underline{\text{Closing}} $. Closing shall take place at the office of the Title Company or at the Seller's offices. If the closing takes place anywhere other than at the office of the Title Company, Seller shall arrange for a Title Company representative with authority to update and mark up the commitment for title insurance as required under this Agreement to be present at the closing. If title can be conveyed in the condition required under this Agreement and all contingencies have been satisfied or waived, closing shall take place on a date and time as is mutually agreeable to the parties, provided, however, that closing shall occur not later than _____ days after the Effective Date (as defined above).

7. Payment of Fees, Closing Costs, Etc. The parties agree that the Title Company shall prepare the required closing documents necessary to complete this transaction, that the Title Company shall conduct the closing, and that the cost of same, together with any settlement, document preparation, disbursement fee shall be borne by the parties, equally. Seller shall pay the required transfer tax. Purchaser shall pay the cost of an owner's commitment and policy of title insurance and applicable recording fees. At closing, the parties shall execute closing statements prepared by the Title Company and all income or other tax reporting documents as required by the Title Company.

<a id="p212"></a>
*[Page 212 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/2-12-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202402090926490#page=212)* (read by OCR; check the original)

8.  $ \underline{\text{Representations and Warranties of Purchaser}} $. Purchaser hereby represents and warrants to Seller that as of the date hereof and on the date of Closing:

A. Purchaser has the full power and authority to execute, deliver and perform this Agreement and all of Purchaser's obligations under this Agreement; and

B. The individual(s) signing this Agreement and all other documents executed or to be executed pursuant hereto on behalf of Purchaser are and shall be duly authorized to sign the same on Purchaser's behalf and to bind Purchaser thereto.

C. Purchaser shall construct the single-family home described in Paragraph 9, below, on the Property in a good and workmanlike manner, employing quality contractors, construction managers, and other professionals possessing the requisite experience and competency to construct such improvements, and in accordance with all applicable laws, ordinances and codes.

9. Conditional Conveyance; Reverter of Property; Escrow of Deed. Purchaser has represented to Seller that it intends to construct a single-family home on the Property in accordance with the Site Plan approved for the development of the Property [INSERT PLAN DATE] ("Site Plan") (attached as Exhibit "B"). Seller materially relied on and placed significant weight on this representation in making its decision to sell the Property to Purchaser at the stated price. Accordingly, Purchaser offered and hereby agrees that the Seller's conveyance of the Property to Purchaser is and shall be expressly conditioned upon the Purchaser completing construction of a single-family home in accordance with the Site Plan attached hereto as Exhibit B, on the Property no later than ___.

In the event Purchaser fails to comply with the foregoing conditions, the Property shall, without any further action, legal or otherwise, revert to Seller. To secure said reversion of the Property in the event Purchaser fails to comply with and satisfy the above conditions, Purchaser shall execute a warranty deed (in the form attached as Exhibit "C") on a date subsequent to the date of the deed signed by Seller conveying the Property to Purchaser but prior to and as a condition of commencement of any construction on the Property, which deed shall be held by the City Attorney in escrow and released according to the following terms: (a) If the conditions set forth above have been satisfied, the deed shall be returned to Purchaser upon Purchaser's written request to the City Attorney, (b) if the conditions set forth above have not been satisfied, the deed shall be recorded with the Oakland County Register of Deeds upon the Seller's written request to the City Attorney, or (c) if the City Attorney has not received a written request from either Seller or Purchaser prior to _____, the deed shall be returned to Purchaser. The address to which written notices to the City Attorney shall be provided under this Paragraph is: Lisa J. Hamameh, Rosati Schultz Joppich & Amtsbuechler, P.C., 27555 Executive Drive, Suite 250, Farmington Hills, MI 48331-3550.

10.  $ \underline{\text{Notices}} $. Any notice required to be given in accordance with the provisions of this Agreement shall be in writing and effective when delivered personally or when mailed by

<a id="p213"></a>
*[Page 213 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/2-12-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202402090926490#page=213)* (read by OCR; check the original)

certified mail, return receipt requested, directed to the parties or attorney in the addresses set forth in this Agreement or at such other address as may be set forth in writing by the respective parties or attorney. It is agreed by the parties that notices required hereunder may, but are not required to, to be delivered by email, provided a hard copy (originally signed copy) is mailed or delivered in a timely manner. If sent by email, the date and time of the receipt of the email shall be the date and time of said notice. If not sent by email, notice shall be deemed provided on the earlier of (a) the date of personal delivery, (b) the date when received, or (c) one day after mailing if mailed in the State of Michigan.

11.  $ \underline{\text{Condition of Premises}} $. Purchaser acknowledges that it is purchasing the vacant Property AS IS. The Purchaser acknowledges that (a) it has examined the Property; (b) it has had the opportunity to conduct additional inspections, examinations and other due diligence for the purchase of the Property; and (c) Seller has not made any representations or warranties of any kind concerning the Property upon which the Purchaser has placed reliance except as provided in this Agreement. Further, Purchaser hereby agrees to release Seller, Seller's employees, officials, councils, consultants and attorneys from any and all claims whatsoever related to the condition of the Property, including any defects in title, environmental contamination, or other conditions.

12.  $ \underline{\text{Headings}} $. The headings contained herein are for the convenience of the parties and are not to be used in construing the provision of this Agreement.

13.  $ \underline{\text{Entire Agreement}} $. Seller and Purchaser agree that this Agreement and the Exhibits attached constitute the entire agreement between them and that there are no agreements, representations, statements or understandings that have been relied upon by them that are not stated in this Agreement.

14.  $ \underline{\text{Binding Effect}} $. The covenants, representations and agreements are binding upon and insure to the benefit of the parties hereto, their respective heirs, representatives, successors and assigns, and Paragraphs 6 through 19 shall survive the closing and conveyance of the Property to Purchaser.

15.  $ \underline{\text{Governing Law}} $. This Agreement shall be governed by and construed in accordance with the statutes and laws of the State of Michigan. In the event that any provision herein shall be held by any court of competent jurisdiction to be illegal or unenforceable, such provision shall be deemed severable and severed therefrom and the remaining provisions herein shall remain in full force and effect between the parties.

16.  $ \underline{\text{Non-Assignability}} $. Purchaser shall not assign, sell or transfer this Agreement or any of its rights, obligations or interests arising hereunder without the prior written consent of

<a id="p214"></a>

Seller, which consent may be withheld for any reason or no reason at all, in the Seller's discretion. Any purported assignment contrary to the terms hereof shall be null, void and have no force and effect, and shall not relive the assignor of its obligations under and pursuant to this Agreement.

17.  $ \underline{\text{Counterparts}} $. This Agreement may be executed in counterparts, and if so executed, the various counterparts shall constitute a single agreement.

18.  $ \underline{\text{Incorporation of Exhibits}} $. The Exhibits attached to this Agreement are incorporated herein and expressly agreed to and made a part of this Agreement for all purposed by this references.

19.  $ \underline{\text{Amendment}} $. This Agreement may not be amended orally, but may only be amended in writing signed by all of the parties.

# AGENDA NOTE New Business #2

MEETING DATE: February 12, 2024

PERSON PLACING ITEM ON AGENDA: City Manager

AGENDA TOPIC: Senior Activity Center Agreement

EXPLANATION OF TOPIC: The City of South Lyon has been working with Lyon Township, Green Oak Township and the South Lyon Community Schools to come up with a new agreement with each of the parties, to support and operate the Center. This agreement reflects funding levels that coincide with the membership of the organization. The amounts charged to each jurisdiction will be in place for a three year period. The Schools have already signed off on the agreement. Its now going to each of the jurisdictions.

MATERIALS ATTACHED AS SUPPORTING DOCUMENTS: Intergovernmental agreement for the Senior Citizen program.

POSSIBLE COURSES OF ACTION: Approve or not approve the new Intergovernmental agreement for the Senior Citizen Program which includes the City of South Lyon, Lyon Township, Green Oak Township and the South Lyon Community Schools

SUGGESTED MOTION: Motion by _____, supported by _____ to approve the new Intergovernmental agreement for the Senior Citizen Program which includes the City of South Lyon, Lyon Township, Green Oak Township and the South Lyon Community Schools

<a id="p225"></a>
*[Page 225 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/2-12-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202402090926490#page=225)* (read by OCR; check the original)

# INTERGOVERNMENTAL AGREEMENT - SENIOR CITIZEN PROGRAM

This Intergovernmental Agreement is made on _____, 2024 by and between the South Lyon Community Schools, whose address is 345 South Warren, South Lyon, Michigan 48178, (“School District”), Green Oak Charter Township, whose address is 10001 Silver Lake Road, Brighton, Michigan, 48116, (“Green Oak”), the Charter Township of Lyon, whose address is 58000 Grand River, New Hudson, Michigan 48165, (“Lyon”), and the City of South Lyon, whose address is 335 S. Warren Street, South Lyon, Michigan 48178, (“South Lyon”) (Green Oak, Lyon, and South Lyon are each a “Municipal Party” and sometimes collectively referred to herein as the “Municipal Parties” and the School District and Municipal Parties are sometimes collectively referred to herein as the “Parties”).

WHEREAS, the Activities or Services for Older Persons Act, MCL 400.571 et seq. declares it a valid public purpose for local units of government to provide services to senior citizens, including actions directed toward the improvement of the social, legal, health, housing, educational, emotional, nutritional, recreational, or mobility status of older persons; and

WHEREAS, the Municipal Parties do not have or operate recreational or other programs for senior citizens who are residents of their communities, and

WHEREAS, the School District is authorized by the Revised School Code, MCL 380.1 et seq., in addition to educating pupils in grades K-12, to provide lifelong education, adult education, community education, training, enrichment, and recreation programs, and

WHEREAS, the School District provides to individuals who are at least fifty (50) years of age (for purposes of this Agreement, referred to as “senior citizens”) and who either reside in the School District or have students attending the School District various educational, recreational, and other programs authorized by the Revised School Code (“Programs”); and

WHEREAS, the Intergovernmental Contracts Between Municipal Corporations Act, MCL 124.1, et seq., authorizes municipal corporations, including cities, townships, and school districts, to jointly provide a service that each has the power to separately perform, and

WHEREAS, the Parties desire to enter into an Agreement whereby the School District will make its Programs available to senior citizens residing within the Municipal Parties, and the Municipal Parties will provide resources to support the operation of such Programs.

NOW, WHEREFORE, the Parties hereby agree as follows:

1. The School District currently operates Programs at the Center for Active Adults (“CAA”), located in the west wing of South Lyon High School, located at 1000 N. Lafayette, South Lyon, Michigan 48178 (“CAA Programs”). The CAA Programs vary, but may include educational classes, enrichment classes, intergenerational projects, information and counseling related to taxes, legal issues, and Medicare, health screenings, and health-related workshops. The School District will provide competent personnel to manage the CAA Programs, which may include hiring licensed and trained personnel to

<a id="p226"></a>
*[Page 226 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/2-12-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202402090926490#page=226)* (read by OCR; check the original)

counsel CAA Program participants. The Municipal Parties will recommend personnel to be hired by the School District to manage and operate CAA Programs. All hires are subject to the hiring process of the School District.

2. The Municipal Parties hereby agree to provide funding to support the CAA Programs, so that such programs are available to senior citizens of these communities at no cost. Funds appropriated by each municipality shall be paid to the School District in two (2) equal installments, the first due sixty (60) days after the membership counts are confirmed on December 31 $ ^{st} $ of the preceding year, with the second bi-annual payment due six (6) months later. In the event a municipality wishes to make a payment for the full year in one lump sum, it may do so. For the first year of the Agreement, the first payment shall be due thirty (30) days following approval of the Agreement by all parties. The Municipal Parties understand that the School District may offer CAA Programs to senior citizens who do not reside within the Municipal Parties' boundaries, and that the School District may charge a fee for such participation. The Municipal Parties are not responsible to pay for CAA Programs for individuals who reside outside of their boundaries.

3. A representative of each Party shall participate in an executive committee to review operations of the CAA, including the budget, and discuss any disputes between the parties or residents and the CAA. Meetings of the executive committee shall occur at least [monthly/bi-annually/annually].

4. Each Municipal Party’s contribution will be based on the pro rata share of its senior citizens participating in CAA Programs. For the initial term of this Agreement, the Municipal Parties’ contributions are as follows:

Green Oak Township $26,800.00

City of South Lyon $56,200.00

Lyon Township $34,000.00

5 The School District shall promote CAA Programs, specifically targeting senior citizens within the Municipal Parties. The School District will endeavor to track participation of senior citizen residents within the Municipal Parties, including for the purpose of determining membership counts and annual payments under Paragraph 2.

6. The School District will maintain facilities and equipment in good repair and condition to operate the CAA Programs, as determined by the School District in its sole discretion and as otherwise required by law. Without limiting the breadth of the foregoing, the Parties acknowledge that facilities and equipment to operate the CAA Programs are obtained and maintained, at least in part, by the payment of annual rent and additional payment for utilities, custodial services, and other indirect expenses. The cost for annual rent is currently $3,000/year, and the cost for utilities, custodial services, and other indirect expenses is currently 8% of total annual CAA Program expenditures, but those amounts are not guaranteed and may fluctuate. If the School District determines that it will no longer maintain or use the CAA facilities or equipment, it will notify the Municipal Parties to discuss next steps, which may include termination of the Agreement.

<a id="p227"></a>
*[Page 227 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/2-12-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202402090926490#page=227)* (read by OCR; check the original)

7.  $ \underline{\text{Insurance}} $.

a. The School District shall obtain and maintain general comprehensive liability insurance in an amount of not less than One Million Dollars ($1,000,000) per occurrence, covering the School District facilities as well as the programs and services provided by CAA.

b. The School District shall maintain workers' compensation insurance if and to the extent required by Michigan law.

8. This Term of this Agreement shall be three (3) years.

9. The Parties shall comply with all applicable federal state and local laws, rules and regulations, including, as applicable, the School District's Board Policies. In addition, all participants of CAA Programs are subject to the School District's Policies and procedures, including with respect to the use of School District facilities.

10. The Municipal Parties agree not to discriminate against any employee or applicant with respect to hiring, tenure, or terms, conditions or privileges of employment, or a matter directly or indirectly related to employment because of race, color, religion, national origin, age, sex, sexual orientation, gender identity, or marital status. The School District further agrees not to discriminate on the above bases in providing CAA Programs.

11. If any Municipal Party intends to commence formal dispute resolution against the School District, it must first provide the School District written notice of the claim and its intent to file an action. No such action shall be commenced prior to the expiration of ninety (90) from the date the School District receives such notification.

12. The Parties designate the following individuals to receive notices and other information related to this Agreement or operation of the CAA Programs:

South Lyon School District: Green Oak Charter Township:

Charter Township of Lyon:

City of South Lyon:

12. No Third-Party Beneficiaries. This Agreement does not and is not intended to create any obligation, duty, promise, contractual right or benefit, right to indemnification, right to subrogation, and/or any other right in favor of any other person or entity.

13. Force Majeure. Each Party shall be excused from any obligations under this Agreement during the time and to the extent that a Party is prevented from performing due to causes beyond the Party's control, including, but not limited to, an act of God, war, fire, strike, labor disputes, civil disturbances, reduction of power source, or any other circumstances beyond

<a id="p228"></a>
*[Page 228 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/2-12-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202402090926490#page=228)* (read by OCR; check the original)

the reasonable control of the affected Party. Without limiting the breadth of the foregoing, CAA Programs will not operate when the School District is closed for operations due to inclement weather (e.g., snow days) or for the School District's scheduled Spring Break. Reasonable notice shall be given to the other Parties for any other force majeure event.

14. Severability. If a court of competent jurisdiction finds a term or condition of this Agreement to be illegal or invalid, then the term or condition shall be deemed severed from this Agreement. All other terms, conditions, and provisions of this Agreement shall remain in full force.

15. Governing Law. This Agreement shall be governed, interpreted, and enforced by the laws of the State of Michigan without giving effect to its conflict of law principles.

16. Entire Agreement. This Agreement sets forth the entire agreement between the Parties. The Prior Agreements and any representations, understandings, statements, negotiations, understandings and undertakings, whether written or oral, in any way concerning or related to the subject matter of this Agreement are fully and completely superseded by this Agreement.

# AGENDA NOTE New Business #3

MEETING DATE: February 12, 2024

PERSON PLACING ITEM ON AGENDA: Patricia Tiernan, Finance and Benefit Administrator

AGENDA TOPIC: Munetrix, LLC 3-Year Subscription Agreement

EXPLANATION OF TOPIC: The City of South Lyon will engage Munetrix, LLC for (3) years to provide a software platform and subscription service for the Municipal Transparency/Financial Performance Platform Dashboard to meet the State of Michigan's Transparency and Accountability Initiative requirements. Munetrix, LLC has been providing this service to the City of South Lyon for over 10 years. This agreement has a 3-year term which will provide the City of South Lyon a 9.4% discount (approximately $925).

MATERIALS ATTACHED AS SUPPORTING DOCUMENTS: Subscription Service Agreement provided by Munetrix, LLC.

POSSIBLE COURSES OF ACTION: Approve or not approve the Subscription Service Agreement by Munetrix, LLC.

# MUNETRIX SUBSCRIPTION AGREEMENT

THIS MUNETRIX SUBSCRIPTION AGREEMENT (the "Agreement") is made by and between MUNETRIX, LLC a Michigan Limited Liability Company whose address for purposes of this Agreement is 3252 University Drive, Suite 170, Auburn Hills, MI 48326 (the "Company") and South Lyon whose address is 335 S. Warren St. South Lyon, MI 48178-1317 (the "Licensee"), (and together with the Company, the "Parties")

WHEREAS, MUNETRIX, LLC, offers subscriptions to the Munetrix Software as a Service Platform as defined on the attached Quote (the "Tool") for the benefit of Administrators, Finance Officers, Business Managers, and Elected Officials, whereby they may subscribe to and be issued rights to use the Tool for fiscal transparency or business management practices;

NOW, THEREFORE, the Parties hereby agree as follows:

1.  $ \underline{\text{Subscription}} $. As a condition to the Agreement, the Licensee shall pay the Company an annual fee (the "License Fee"). Payment of the License Fee is due within thirty (30) days of the earlier of a) the date Company sends an invoice to Licensee for the License Fee or b) Licensee's registration for use of the Tool. Within a commercially reasonable time following the Company's receipt of the executed Subscription Agreement, the Company agrees to provide the Licensee with full access to editing rights to the Munetrix Tool through the Company's website (Munetrix.com).

The annual subscription will have a start date as stated in the Attached Quote under Contract Start Date ("Start Date") and shall continue for the Contract Period as indicated on the Attached Quote ("Term"). The Contract End date on the attached Quote will be the subscription end date ("End Date").

The Parties agree that the Term shall automatically be renewed for successive one-year periods after the first twelve (12) month term expires ("Renewal Term") unless the Company is notified in writing by the Licensee at least thirty (30) days prior to the applicable End Date that Licensee intends not to allow the Term to automatically renew. Licensee shall pay the Company a subsequent License Fee for each Renewal Term within thirty (30) days of the date Company sends an invoice to Licensee for the additional License Fee. The Licensee acknowledges that the License Fee may increase in each renewal period. The Company agrees to notify the Licensee at least sixty (60) days prior to a new Renewal Term if any increase in the License Fee will be implemented for that Renewal Term, and if a "Rate Lock"; option is available.

2. Users. For the purposes of this Agreement, the term "Authorized Users" shall mean any chief finance officers, chief administrators, support staff, or other employees of the Licensee that are elected, appointed and/or designated by the Licensee's Chief Administrative Officer during the Term of the Agreement.

3.  $ \underline{\text{Licensee and User's Responsibilities}} $. The Licensee agrees that each Authorized User shall (i) be responsible for the accuracy, quality and legality of its data entered into the Tool (or any database that utilizes the Tool) and of the means by which such data is acquired, (ii) use commercially reasonable efforts to prevent unauthorized access to or use of the Tool, and notify the Company promptly of any such unauthorized access or use, and (iii) use the Tool only in accordance with the Terms of Use and applicable laws and government regulations.

The Licensee agrees that each Authorized User shall not (a) make the Tool available to anyone other than an Authorized User, unless otherwise allowed or authorized per this Agreement, (b) sell, resell, rent or lease the Tool, (c) use the Tool to store or transmit infringing, libelous, or otherwise unlawful or tortious material, or to store or transmit material in violation of third-party privacy rights or any other applicable law, (d) use the Tool to store or transmit viruses or malicious code, (e) interfere with or disrupt the integrity or performance of the Tool or any third-party data contained therein, or (f) attempt to gain unauthorized access to the Tool or Company's related systems or networks.

The Licensee agrees to immediately discontinue, decommission or otherwise notify Company to decommission any Authorized User from having access to the Tool upon termination of their employment from the Licensee's organization.

4.  $ \underline{\text{Company Responsibilities}} $ At a minimum of once per year, and as long as state agencies continue to provide Company access to bulk data downloads, the Company agrees to keep the data available to the Tool current with the most recent data made accessible to Company. The Company will also maintain the Tool in terms of security and up-time in accordance with industry standards in place at the signing of this Agreement.

Basic system enhancements will occur periodically at no additional charge. These updates will be in the form of slight modifications that make the system more user-friendly or intuitive and may include various additions to data or charts that can help a governmental entity, or the general public understand the data better. No version

downloads are required by the Licensee; these updates will automatically appear once released for production and a user notification will appear upon the Licensees next log-in. The notification feature is turned "on" as the Tool's default setting, and this preference may be changed by the Licensee using the system's settings.

5. Terms of Use. The Licensee agrees to be bound by (and comply with) and agrees to cause its Authorized Users to be bound by (and comply with) this Agreement and Munetrix's Terms of Use. For the purpose of this Agreement, the "Terms of Use" shall mean those certain Terms of Use that are set forth on Munetrix's website (munetrix.com) and relate to the usage of Munetrix's site, tools, and products, as modified from time to time. To the extent that there is any direct conflict between the terms of this Agreement and the Terms of Use, the terms of this Agreement shall control and prevail.

6. Guest User Link. As a condition to this Agreement, the Licensee agrees to place an approved Munetrix Citizens Guide to Finances guest user link or other means to hyperlink the Munetrix Tool to their websites homepage.

7.  $ \underline{\text{Limitation of Liability}} $. The liability of the Company arising out of or related to this Agreement or the Tool shall not exceed the amount of the most recent License Fee paid by the Licensee under this Agreement.

8.  $ \underline{\text{Relationship of the Parties}} $. The Parties to this Agreement are independent contractors. This Agreement does not create a partnership, franchise, joint venture, agency, fiduciary or employment relationship between the Parties.

9.  $ \underline{\text{Assignment}} $. Licensee may not assign any of its rights or delegate any of its duties under this Agreement without the prior written consent of the Company.

10.  $ \underline{\text{Severability}} $. If any provision of this Agreement is held by a court of competent jurisdiction to be contrary to law the provision shall be modified by the court and interpreted so as best to accomplish the objectives of the original provision to the fullest extent permitted by law and the remaining provisions of this Agreement shall remain in effect.

In addition to any other rights and remedies that the Parties might have at law or in equity, the Company reserves the right to suspend or cancel this Agreement and access to the Tool in the event that the Licensee breaches (or threatens to breach) any term of this Agreement.

11. Counterparts. This Agreement may be executed in counterparts, each of which shall be deemed an original, but all of which together shall be deemed to be one and the same agreement. A signed copy of this Agreement delivered by facsimile, e-mail, or other means of electronic transmission shall be deemed to have the same legal effect as delivery of an original signed copy of this Agreement. Use of the system is also deemed an acceptance of the agreement.

12.  $ \underline{\text{Covering Law}} $. This Agreement shall be bound by the laws of the State of Michigan without regard to its conflicts of law provisions.

13.  $ \underline{\text{Entire Agreement}} $. This Agreement along with the Terms of Use constitutes the entire agreement between the Parties and supersedes all prior and contemporaneous agreements, proposals or representations, written or oral, concerning its subject matter. No modification, amendment, or waiver of any provision of this Agreement shall be effective unless in writing and signed by the party against whom the modification, amendment or waiver is to be asserted.

The Licensee agrees to the provisions of this agreement and by signing warrants and represents that they have the authority to execute the transaction as of the Start Date.

South Lyon

## MEMO

*[Image or figure on this page; see the original.]*

Date: February 8, 2024

To: City Council

From: Paul C. Zelenak, Manager

Re: City Update

Future Agendas: Fire Department and Police Department yearly reports, and the Washington Manor Development.

Please retain information from the January 22 $ ^{nd} $ Council meeting packet pertaining to the Westlake Development Project. This will eliminate the need to copy all the same information (plans, review letters, etc.) for your packet when this comes back before City Council on Monday. This new submittal is based upon the discussion and direction that was given to the applicant at the City Council meeting on January 22 $ ^{nd} $

I railway / Sidewalk improvements: I had a meeting last week with the township to discuss improvements that would affect both of our railway systems. We discussed partnering to put in a crosswalk signal on 11 Mile Road and also potentially adding segments of sidewalk on 10 mile near Dixboro and also on Martindale. We would include this amount in the 2024-2025 budget suggestions.

Knolls Development: This week I met with our engineer, our attorney and with the developers of the Knolls. We are working to get the final items completed by the developer before the roads and utilities can be accepted by the City.

Road Improvement Project: We held the open-house meeting with the residents of Brookfield and Eagle Pointe. Approximately 25 people attended. They were very excited about the upcoming road projects. We will hold a secondary meeting after we go out and award the bid for the project. This meeting will be held in late April or early May.

## CODE ENFORCEMENT

## New Enforcements

## New Enforcements

521 W. Lake – Blight: The ordinance officer was advised to check on this property because the roof has been tarped since a tree fell on it this past summer. The ordinance officer spoke with the property owner, who said the roof repair will be done in the next 30 days. When advised that there was no permit for the work, he said he would be in to obtain the permit himself.

62516 Raleigh #2 – Permit: The ordinance officer was informed by the building department that the mechanical inspector was at this address for a water heater inspection and observed a new kitchen sink install and a new bath install, both of which require updated plumbing that was not done. A check in the building department system showed no permits were obtained for this work. The ordinance officer will attempt to contact the property owner to advise them a permit is needed.

## Closed Enforcements

505 E. Lake – Sign: The electronic sign passed a final inspection and the sign was changed to a steady mode from the flashing and changing that was occurring when the complaint came in.

## Mutual Aid Agreement Between City of South Lyon & Green Oak Charter TWP:

An agreement will be brought to the City Council for approval at the next city council meeting, February 12^{th}.

The agreement is attached for your review.

The Washtenaw Area Mutual Aid Association (WAMAA) raised their annual membership dues. Because WAMAA emergency services/rescue teams provided duplicate services that Oakland County also provides, it

was decided not to re-new the SLFD membership at the end of the last calendar year. Automatic mutual aid services that were previously covered between The City of South Lyon Fire and Green Oak Charter Township Fire Departments through WAMAA now need a new standalone agreement. This new agreement does not change the way the two fire departments have been working together.

# DDA-ED Report for February 9, 2024

The winter farmers' market is returning this weekend, February 10 from 10 AM – 2 PM.

The DDA Board met Thursday, February 8. During the meeting, the board discussed and recommended approval of the DDA's budget for the 2024-2025 fiscal year. The board also accepted Mark Childs' resignation and recommended Daniel Gyomory be appointed for the remainder of Mr. Childs' term.

Mark Childs was a long-time DDA board member. He was on the board when the DDA was originally established in the early 2000s. South Lyon Cycle has been a downtown South Lyon business for 30 years as well.

- The DDA Director has started the process for the farmers' market to accept SNAP this season. I met the Michigan Farmers' Market Association this week, and they informed me we may be able to accept SNAP this season. There is also the opportunity for us to receive a point-of-sale system to accept EBT at no cost to the DDA.

The Law Office of Julie Paquette will be having a five-year anniversary celebration on Friday, March 15. There will be a ribbon cutting at 4:30 PM for their South Lyon office.

Main Street Oakland County is working with South Lyon, New Hudson/Lyon Township, Wixom, and Highland "south x southwest" exchange group for a chance to grow a peer-to-peer network in our area of the county around downtown development. The first meeting of this group will be taking place in South Lyon. The overall goal is to exchange information, ideas, and build subregional networks focused on the work on the Main Street Approach.

The DDA Director has a meeting with Oakland County regarding the funding disbursement for the grant received for Lafayette Park later this month. The county will be seeking information on how the different communities' projects are shaping up, and they will be providing an update on how the funds will be disbursed (reimbursement versus up-front payment) and the timing of the interlocal agreement.

- The DDA Director has spoken with Randy Clark regarding the gazebo on Bifano's property. The gazebo is not historically significant and there is no reason it cannot be removed to allow Bifano to have a regular driveway as opposed to one that is not split. The City will remove the plaque from the gazebo and Bifano will raze the gazebo as they begin the work to renovate their building.
